After preprocessing, fiber orientation distributions (FODs) were estimated for each subject using the Multi-Shell Multi-Tissue Constrained Spherical Deconvolution (MSMT-CSD) algorithm (Jeurissen et al., 2014) using group averaged response functions for white matter, grey matter, and CSF (Dhollander et al., 2016). To create a whole-brain tractogram using Anatomically Constrained Tractography (ACT) algorithm, tissue classification for 5 different tissue types (cortical gray matter, subcortical gray matter, white matter, CSF and pathological tissue) were generated from the anatomical data. Next, anatomical images were co-registered to averaged b0 using FLIRT toolbox of FSL. Analysis of five million streamlines was performed over the entire brain through probabilistic tractography, which was implemented in the MRtrix. The cortex parcellation with 7 networks for each hemisphere (200 parcels) (Schaefer et al., 2018) was used to generate the structural connectome including the default mode network (DMN; 27 nodes), the limbic network (6 nodes), the visual network (14 nodes), the somatomotor network (SMN;16 nodes), the frontoparietal network (13 nodes), the dorsal attention network (DAN; 13 nodes), and the ventral attention network (VAN; 11 nodes) (supplementary Table 1). We added 14 subcortical areas to the atlas using the FIRST command in FSL software. For each participant, 214 × 214 connectivity matrices were derived using the number of streamlines connecting between each pair of regions, where 214 is the total number of nodes. The COMMIT2 algorithm was then applied to remove false positive streamlines and filter the raw connectome. Finally, the raw and COMMIT-weighted connectomes were constructed for each subject\" and add these sentences to it :In this study, we utilized a methodology for generating the structural connectome and constructing tractography-based connectomes using diffusion-weighted images (DWIs) in MRtrix. The basic principle for generating the structural connectome involved first preprocessing the DWIs to correct any distortions and register them to the anatomical image. Next, we performed tractography using the MRtrix software to create fiber streamlines in the brain. These streamlines represent the white matter pathways connecting different regions of the brain. From these streamlines, we calculated the connectivity matrix, which provides information on the strength and directionality of the connections between different brain regions. To construct tractography-based connectomes, we utilized the connectivity matrix and a network graph theory approach. This involved defining nodes representing different brain regions based on a parcellation map, and edges representing the connectivity between the nodes. We applied graph theory measures to analyze the topology of the network and identify any changes in connectivity patterns.", "criteria": [{"criterion_id": "1", "question": "是否完整保留了原文所有关键技术步骤、软件工具名称（MRtrix3, FSL, ANTs）、具体算法（Veraart denoising, EPI distortion correction, motion/eddy correction, bias field correction, MSMT-CSD, ACT, FLIRT, COMMIT2）及对应的文献引用？"}, {"criterion_id": "2", "question": "是否成功融入了新增段落的核心概念，包括 DWI 预处理目的、纤维流形线的生物学定义、连通矩阵的计算方式以及基于图论的连接体构建与分析方法？"}, {"criterion_id": "3", "question": "重写后的文本是否在逻辑上实现了从数据预处理到最终连接体重建的顺畅过渡，且消除了两段原始文本直接拼接可能产生的冗余或断裂感？"}, {"criterion_id": "4", "question": "是否准确保留了原文中关于脑区划分的具体参数（7 个网络共 200 个节点、14 个皮层下区域、总计 214 个节点）及各网络的名称和节点数？"}, {"criterion_id": "5", "question": "文本的整体语言风格是否符合学术论文的方法论部分规范，用词专业且表达清晰？"}], "response": "In this study, we utilized a methodology for generating the structural connectome and constructing tractography-based connectomes using diffusion-weighted images (DWIs) in MRtrix. After preprocessing, fiber orientation distributions (FODs) were estimated for each subject using the Multi-Shell Multi-Tissue Constrained Spherical Deconvolution (MSMT-CSD) algorithm (Jeurissen et al., 2014) using group averaged response functions for white matter, grey matter, and CSF (Dhollander et al., 2016). To create a whole-brain tractogram using Anatomically Constrained Tractography (ACT) algorithm, tissue classification for 5 different tissue types (cortical gray matter, subcortical gray matter, white matter, CSF and pathological tissue) were generated from the anatomical data. After preprocessing, fiber orientation distributions (FODs) were estimated for each subject using the Multi-Shell Multi-Tissue Constrained Spherical Deconvolution (MSMT-CSD) algorithm (Jeurissen et al., 2014) using group averaged response functions for white matter, grey matter, and CSF (Dhollander et al., 2016). To create a whole-brain tractogram using Anatomically Constrained Tractography (ACT) algorithm, tissue classification for 5 different tissue types (cortical gray matter, subcortical gray matter, white matter, CSF and pathological tissue) were generated from the anatomical data. Next, anatomical images were co-registered to averaged b0 using FLIRT toolbox of FSL. Analysis of five million streamlines was performed over the entire brain through probabilistic tractography, which was implemented in the MRtrix. The cortex parcellation with 7 networks for each hemisphere (200 parcels) (Schaefer et al., 2018) was used to generate the structural connectome including the default mode network (DMN; 27 nodes), the limbic network (6 nodes), the visual network (14 nodes), the somatomotor network (SMN;16 nodes), the frontoparietal network (13 nodes), the dorsal attention network (DAN; 13 nodes), and the ventral attention network (VAN; 11 nodes) (supplementary Table 1). We added 14 subcortical areas to the atlas using the FIRST command in FSL software. For each participant, 214 × 214 connectivity matrices were derived using the number of streamlines connecting between each pair of regions, where 214 is the total number of nodes. The COMMIT2 algorithm was then applied to remove false positive streamlines and filter the raw connectome. The basic principle for generating the structural connectome involved first preprocessing the DWIs to correct any distortions and register them to the anatomical image. Next, we performed tractography using the MRtrix software to create fiber streamlines in the brain. These streamlines represent the white matter pathways connecting different regions of the brain. From these streamlines, we calculated the connectivity matrix, which provides information on the strength and directionality of the connections between different brain regions. To construct tractography-based connectomes, we utilized the connectivity matrix and a network graph theory approach. This involved defining nodes representing different brain regions based on a parcellation map, and edges representing the connectivity between the nodes. The process began with preprocessing the DWIs, which included: denoising the data (Veraart et al., 2016) using MRtrix3, correction of EPI distortions (Holland et al., 2010), correction for motion and eddy-current distortions (Andersson and Sotiropoulos, 2016) using FSL, and bias field correction (Tustison et al., 2010) using ANTs. After preprocessing, fiber orientation distributions (FODs) were estimated for each subject using the Multi-Shell Multi-Tissue Constrained Spherical Deconvolution (MSMT-CSD) algorithm (Jeurissen et al., 2014) with group averaged response functions for white matter, grey matter, and CSF (Dhollander et al., 2016).\n\nTo create a whole-brain tractogram, tissue classification for five different tissue types (cortical gray matter, subcortical gray matter, white matter, CSF, and pathological tissue) was generated from the anatomical data. Anatomical images were then co-registered to averaged b0 using the FLIRT toolbox of FSL. Analysis of five million streamlines was performed over the entire brain through probabilistic tractography, which was implemented in MRtrix. This step generated fiber streamlines representing the white matter pathways connecting different regions of the brain.\n\nThe cortex parcellation with seven networks for each hemisphere (200 parcels) (Schaefer et al., 2018) was used to define nodes representing different brain regions. We added 14 subcortical areas to the atlas using the FIRST command in FSL software, resulting in a total of 214 nodes. For each participant, 214 × 214 connectivity matrices were derived using the number of streamlines connecting between each pair of regions. The COMMIT2 algorithm was then applied to remove false positive streamlines and filter the raw connectome.\n\nTo construct tractography-based connectomes, we utilized the connectivity matrix and a network graph theory approach. This involved defining edges representing the connectivity between the nodes based on the number of streamlines connecting each pair of regions. We applied graph theory measures to analyze the topology of the network and identify any changes in connectivity patterns. After preprocessing, fiber orientation distributions (FODs) were estimated for each subject using the Multi-Shell Multi-Tissue Constrained Spherical Deconvolution (MSMT-CSD) algorithm (Jeurissen et al., 2014) using group averaged response functions for white matter, grey matter, and CSF (Dhollander et al., 2016). While knowing precisely what these weights are might seem helpful or even essential, such knowledge is not as useful as you might believe—just why is explained below.\nHow Much Each Competitive Factor Matters Is Not a Fixed Amount. Both logic and common business sense instruct that price is certainly a very important factor affecting both a company’s competitiveness against rivals and overall buyer appeal for one brand versus another. Big price differences in a region matter a lot in accounting for differences in branded pairs sold and market share. But as the spread between the highest-priced company and the lowest-priced company becomes smaller and smaller, the weaker is the unit sales/market share impact of price differences and the greater the role of differences on other competitive factors in causing the sales and market shares of some companies in the region to differ from those of other companies. Big price differences in a region matter a lot in accounting for differences in branded pairs sold and market share. Theo quy luật chung, ba yếu tố cạnh tranh quan trọng nhất ảnh hưởng đến sự lựa chọn thương hiệu của người mua, số lượng đôi giày bán ra và thị phần là giá (giá bán buôn trung bình trong trường hợp đôi giày có thương hiệu được bán bởi các đại lý bán lẻ giày và giá trung bình trực tuyến trong trường hợp đôi giày được mua trực tuyến), xếp hạng S/Q và số lượng mẫu/mẫu mã được cung cấp. Các yếu tố có ảnh hưởng tiếp theo bao gồm sức hút của các người nổi tiếng ủng hộ các thương hiệu đối thủ, quảng cáo thương hiệu, quảng cáo tìm kiếm, hình ảnh công ty/danh tiếng thương hiệu, số lượng đại lý bán lẻ kinh doanh thương hiệu giày của công ty và giao hàng miễn phí. Ưu thế/nhược điểm cạnh tranh liên quan đến sự khác biệt về số tuần để giao hàng cho đơn đặt hàng của đại lý bán lẻ, kích thước của các khoản hoàn tiền gửi và số lượng hỗ trợ tương đối được cung cấp cho đại lý bán lẻ trong việc trưng bày và quảng bá thương hiệu của họ có trọng lượng nhẹ nhất trong việc xác định sự lựa chọn thương hiệu của người mua và doanh số bán hàng theo đơn vị khu vực và thị phần của mỗi công ty.\n\nTrọng số được đặt đằng sau các yếu tố cạnh tranh 13 phần lớn phản ánh những gì được cho là thực sự tồn tại trên thị trường thực tế cho giày thể thao. Mặc dù việc biết chính xác những trọng số này có vẻ hữu ích hoặc thậm chí là cần thiết, nhưng kiến thức đó không hữu ích như bạn nghĩ - lý do chính xác được giải thích bên dưới.\n\nMức độ quan trọng của mỗi yếu tố cạnh tranh không phải là một con số cố định. Cả logic và kinh doanh thông thường đều chỉ ra rằng giá là một yếu tố rất quan trọng ảnh hưởng đến cả sự cạnh tranh của một công ty đối với các đối thủ cạnh tranh và sự hấp dẫn chung của người mua đối với một thương hiệu này so với thương hiệu khác. Sự khác biệt giá lớn trong một khu vực rất quan trọng trong việc giải thích sự khác biệt về số lượng đôi giày có thương hiệu được bán ra và thị phần. Nhưng khi khoảng cách giữa công ty có giá cao nhất và công ty có giá thấp nhất ngày càng nhỏ lại, tác động của sự khác biệt về giá đối với doanh số bán hàng/thị phần sẽ yếu đi và vai trò của sự khác biệt về các yếu tố cạnh tranh khác trong việc gây ra sự khác biệt về doanh số bán hàng và thị phần của một số công ty trong khu vực so với các công ty khác sẽ lớn hơn. Thật vậy, trong trường hợp hiếm hoi mà tất cả các công ty đều có thể tính giá bán buôn giống nhau cho đại lý bán lẻ trong một khu vực và giá trực tuyến giống nhau trên các trang web của họ, thì giá trở thành một yếu tố cạnh tranh hoàn toàn không tồn tại và không có tác động nào đến sự hấp dẫn của người mua đối với một thương hiệu này so với thương hiệu khác - trong những trường hợp như vậy, 100% sự khác biệt về doanh số bán hàng và thị phần khu vực giữa các đối thủ cạnh tranh trực tiếp bắt nguồn từ sự khác biệt về 12 yếu tố cạnh tranh khác. Do đó, mức độ giá quan trọng trong việc xác định doanh số bán hàng/thị phần của một công ty trong một khu vực không phải là một con số cố định mà là một con số thay đổi từ \"lớn\" (khi sự khác biệt về giá cũng \"lớn\") đến \"nhỏ\" (khi sự khác biệt về giá là \"nhỏ\") đến \"không\" (khi giá của các đối thủ cạnh tranh là giống nhau).\n\nLý luận chính xác tương tự cũng áp dụng cho 12 yếu tố cạnh tranh khác. As a general rule, the three most important competitive factors affecting buyer brand preferences, pairs sold, and market shares are price (the average wholesale price in the case of branded pairs sold by footwear retailers and the average online price in the case of pairs purchased online), S/Q ratings, and the number of models/styles offered. The next most influential factors include the appeal of celebrities endorsing the various rival brands, brand advertising, search engine advertising, company image/brand reputation, the number of retail outlets stocking the company’s brand of footwear, and free shipping. The competitive advantages/disadvantages associated with differences in the number of weeks it takes to deliver retailer orders, the sizes of mail-in rebates, and the comparative amounts of support provided to retailers in merchandising and promoting their respective brands weigh the least heavily in determining buyer brand preferences and each company’s regional unit sales and market share.\\nThe behind-the-scenes weights placed on the 13 competitive factors closely mirror what is believed to actually prevail in the real-world marketplace for athletic footwear. While knowing precisely what these weights are might seem helpful or even essential, such knowledge is not as useful as you might believe—just why is explained below.\\nHow Much Each Competitive Factor Matters Is Not a Fixed Amount. Both logic and common business sense instruct that price is certainly a very important factor affecting both a company’s competitiveness against rivals and overall buyer appeal for one brand versus another. Big price differences in a region matter a lot in accounting for differences in branded pairs sold and market share. But as the spread between the highest-priced company and the lowest-priced company becomes smaller and smaller, the weaker is the unit sales/market share impact of price differences and the greater the role of differences on other competitive factors in causing the sales and market shares of some companies in the region to differ from those of other companies. Indeed, in the rare instance that all companies should happen to charge the same wholesale price to retailers in a region and the same online price at their websites, then price becomes a total competitive nonfactor and has zero impact on buyer appeal for one brand versus another—in such cases, 100% of the regional sales and market share differences among company rivals stem directly from differences on the other 12 competitive factors. Therefore, how much price matters in determining a company’s unit sales/market share in a region is not a fixed amount but rather is an amount that varies from “big” (when price differences are also “big) to “small” (when prices differences are “small”) to “zero” (when the prices of rivals are identical).\\nPrecisely the same reasoning holds for all the other 12 competitive factors. While it is true that some competitive factors affect the brand choices of buyers more than others, what matters most in determining sales and market shares is the sizes of the differentials on each competitive factor. Big differences on a less influential competitive factor (like the number of weeks in which orders are delivered to retailers) can end up having a bigger sales/market share impact than very small/insignificant differences on more influential competitive factors (like price, S/Q rating, and advertising).\\\"\", \"criteria\": [{\"criterion_id\": \"1\", \"question\": \"译文是否完整且准确地列出了原文提及的全部13个竞争因素，并正确区分了高影响力（价格、S/Q评级、款式数量）、中影响力（名人代言、广告等）和低影响力（配送周数、返利、零售支持）三个层级？\"}, {\"criterion_id\": \"2\", \"question\": \"译文是否精准还原了原文关于‘竞争因素权重非固定值’的核心逻辑，即清晰表达了因素影响力取决于‘差异幅度大小’而非绝对数值，并准确传达了价格差异趋近于零时其权重归零的特殊情形？\"}, {\"criterion_id\": \"3\", \"question\": \"译文中的关键商业术语（如'wholesale price', 'online price', 'S/Q ratings', 'mail-in rebates', 'merchandising', 'market shares'等）是否在越南语中使用了符合行业惯例的标准译法？\"}, {\"criterion_id\": \"4\", \"question\": \"译文是否保持了原文正式、客观的商业分析语调，句子结构符合越南语语法习惯，无生硬直译导致的歧义或逻辑断裂？\"}, {\"criterion_id\": \"5\", \"question\": \"译文是否完整保留了原文的所有段落结构、连接词逻辑（如'Therefore', 'Indeed', 'While knowing...'）以及解释性背景，未擅自删减或添加信息？\"}], \"response\": \"## Dịch tiếng Việt:\\n\\nNhư dự kiến, 13 yếu tố cạnh tranh đối với giày thể thao có tác động khác nhau - một số yếu tố có trọng lượng lớn hơn những yếu tố khác trong việc xác định mức độ ảnh hưởng của sự khác biệt giữa các công ty đối với sự lựa chọn thương hiệu của người mua, số lượng đôi giày có thương hiệu được bán và thị phần. Indeed, in the rare instance that all companies should happen to charge the same wholesale price to retailers in a region and the same online price at their websites, then price becomes a total competitive nonfactor and has zero impact on buyer appeal for one brand versus another—in such cases, 100% of the regional sales and market share differences among company rivals stem directly from differences on the other 12 competitive factors. Therefore, how much price matters in determining a company’s unit sales/market share in a region is not a fixed amount but rather is an amount that varies from “big” (when price differences are also “big) to “small” (when prices differences are “small”) to “zero” (when the prices of rivals are identical).\\n\\nPrecisely the same reasoning holds for all the other 12 competitive factors. While it is true that some competitive factors affect the brand choices of buyers more than others, what matters most in determining sales and market shares is the sizes of the differentials on each competitive factor. Big differences on a less influential competitive factor (like the number of weeks in which orders are delivered to retailers) can end up having a bigger sales/market share impact than very
